考研英语精读 · No.67

The Cave Found by a Dog

几个少年和一只狗发现的洞穴

Marcel Ravidat, 18, was on a woodland stroll near Montignac with Robot, his canine companion, when it began sniffing at a hollow left by a toppled tree. On 12 September 1940 he came back with three other boys for an adventurous excursion, and the four explorers climbed down into the darkness.

18岁的马塞尔·拉维达带着他的犬类伙伴罗伯特在蒙蒂尼亚克附近的林地里散步，这时罗伯特开始在一棵倒伏的树留下的坑洞边嗅来嗅去。1940年9月12日，他带着另外三个男孩回到这里，来了一次富于冒险的出游，四个探险者爬进了黑暗之中。

In later retellings, Ravidat embroidered his anecdote with a dramatic twist. Robot, he said, had fallen into the cave. That fanciful mishap is a fabrication. The animal only nosed about the hole, and the pivotal initiative was the boys' own. Ironically, the invented fall made the dog the hero of the story.

在后来的复述中，拉维达给这段轶事添油加醋，加入了一个戏剧性的转折，说罗伯特掉进了洞里。这个离奇的意外纯属编造。那只狗只是在洞口嗅了嗅，关键的主动行动完全出自几个男孩。讽刺的是，虚构的那一跤让狗成了故事的主角。

Some 680 painted figures and 1,500 engravings cover the rock. The artwork is usually dated to between 17,000 and 22,000 years ago; its creators, our distant ancestors, lived in antiquity. Horses, bulls and stags are among the animals depicted in pigments on the murals. The paintings' fragility soon became clear.

约680个彩绘形象和1,500幅雕刻图案布满岩壁。这些艺术作品通常被认为距今1.7万至2.2万年；它们的创作者是我们遥远的祖先，生活在远古时代。马、公牛和雄鹿都是壁画上用颜料描绘的动物。这些画作的脆弱很快就显现出来。

Lascaux opened to the public on 14 July 1948, and tourism brought an influx of about 1,200 visitors a day. The throng exhaled carbon dioxide and added heat, humidity and contaminants, and the deterioration of the paintings became plain to see. In 1963 the site closed to the public, followed by restoration and daily inspection.

1948年7月14日，拉斯科向公众开放，旅游业带来了每天约1,200名游客的涌入。人群呼出二氧化碳，带来热量、湿气和污染物，壁画的退化变得肉眼可见。1963年，洞穴对公众关闭，随后进行了修复，并开始每日检查。

Lascaux joined UNESCO's World Heritage List in 1979, and Lascaux II, a meticulously made partial facsimile, opened in 1983. A white fungus invaded the walls in a 2001 outbreak, and black stains spread after chemical treatment. Lascaux IV opened in December 2016. Admirers now meet the bulls only in a recreation, the originals being inaccessible to them.

1979年，拉斯科被列入联合国教科文组织《世界遗产名录》；1983年，精心制作的局部复制品"拉斯科二号"开放。2001年，一种白色真菌爆发，侵入洞壁；化学处理后，黑色污斑蔓延开来。2016年12月，"拉斯科四号"开放。如今仰慕者只能在复原品中见到那些公牛，原洞对他们来说无法进入。

考研英语精读 · No.68

The Last Flight of the Hindenburg

兴登堡号的 34 秒

In 1936 the colossal Hindenburg made 17 Atlantic round trips, a marvel of luxury and glamour. Its amenities included a lounge and, that year, a grand piano. An American embargo on helium export had left the ship relying on hydrogen. Because that gas was combustible, smokers sat behind an airlock with one electric lighter.

1936年，巨大的兴登堡号完成了17次跨大西洋往返飞行，是奢华与魅力的奇观。它的设施包括一间休息厅，那一年还有一架三角钢琴。美国对氦气出口实行禁运，这艘飞艇只能依靠氢气。由于氢气易燃，吸烟者只能坐在一道气闸后面，那里只有一个电点火器。

The airship left Frankfurt on 3 May 1937. Hours behind its timetable, it met thunderstorms at Lakehurst and circled for over an hour before its descent towards the mooring mast. At about 7:25 pm on 6 May, flames appeared near the stern. The inferno incinerated the ship in about half a minute, leaving a crumpled frame.

1937年5月3日，飞艇离开法兰克福。它比时刻表晚了几个小时，在莱克赫斯特遇到雷暴，盘旋了一个多小时，才开始向系留塔架下降。5月6日晚约7时25分，飞艇尾部附近出现了火焰。这场烈火在约半分钟内把飞艇焚毁，只留下一副扭曲变形的骨架。

Of the 97 aboard, 62 survived, a proportion close to two-thirds, while a minority of 35 died, as did one man on the ground. A 14-year-old cabin boy was drenched by a deluge from a burst water tank, and the soaking saved him. He dropped through a hatch and scrambled clear, escaping only narrowly.

艇上97人中有62人生还，比例接近三分之二；遇难的35人是少数，地面上另有一名男子丧生。一名14岁的侍应生被一个破裂水箱倾泻而下的水浇透，正是这一身湿透救了他。他从一个舱口钻下去，连滚带爬地逃开，侥幸逃过一劫。

Newsreel cameras and photographers caught the disaster. A radio correspondent's eyewitness commentary was not live; audiences heard it the next day. The cause has not been definitively established, but static electricity igniting leaking hydrogen is the leading explanation. A theory blaming the doped fabric has been rejected.

新闻纪录片摄影机和摄影师拍下了这场灾难。一位电台通讯员的目击解说并不是直播，听众第二天才听到。起火原因至今没有确切查明，但最主要的解释是静电点燃了泄漏的氢气。一种归咎于上过涂料的蒙皮织物的说法已被否定。

The 1933 crash of USS Akron killed 73 yet won less notoriety. The filmed fire left a fateful image that abruptly wrecked confidence. As public perception soured, Graf Zeppelin was withdrawn, later scrapped, and a glamorous epoch of aviation closed. The doomed airship's first journey to North America had begun on 6 May 1936, exactly a year before the fire.

1933年"阿克伦号"失事造成73人死亡，恶名却没那么大。被拍下的大火留下了一幅影响深远的画面，骤然摧毁了人们的信心。公众观感变差后，"格拉夫·齐柏林号"退役，后来被拆毁，航空史上一个光彩夺目的时代就此结束。这艘终将毁于大火的飞艇首次飞往北美的航程始于1936年5月6日，恰好在那场大火的一年前。

考研英语精读 · No.69

The Trial of the Father of the Atomic Bomb

原子弹之父的审判

J. Robert Oppenheimer, born in New York on 22 April 1904, was an accomplished physicist and prominent lecturer at Berkeley and Caltech. From April 1943 he oversaw a secretive weapons installation at Los Alamos, whose bombs were the collaborative creation of a thousands-strong workforce.

J·罗伯特·奥本海默1904年4月22日生于纽约，是一位卓有成就的物理学家，也是伯克利和加州理工学院的知名教师。从1943年4月起，他主管洛斯阿拉莫斯的一处秘密武器设施；这里的炸弹，是数千人的员工队伍协作的成果。

In the pre-dawn darkness of 16 July 1945, the first nuclear test illuminated the desolate New Mexico landscape with a blinding glare, its explosive force roughly 20 kilotons. On 6 and 9 August 1945 atomic bombings devastated Hiroshima and Nagasaki, where year-end fatalities numbered well over 100,000 and about 70,000 respectively.

1945年7月16日黎明前的黑暗中，第一次核试验以刺眼的强光照亮了新墨西哥州荒凉的大地，其爆炸威力约合2万吨TNT当量。1945年8月6日和9日，原子弹轰炸摧毁了广岛和长崎，到当年年底，两地的死亡人数分别远超十万和约七万。

Oppenheimer left Los Alamos in October 1945 and from 1947 presided over Princeton's Institute for Advanced Study. In October 1949 the advisory committee he chaired, expressing misgivings, unanimously counselled against a crash programme for the hydrogen bomb, a vastly more destructive weapon. In December 1953 a presidential directive severed his access to confidential documentation.

1945年10月，奥本海默离开洛斯阿拉莫斯；从1947年起，他执掌普林斯顿高等研究院。1949年10月，他主持的顾问委员会表达了疑虑，一致建议不要推行氢弹的仓促研制计划；氢弹的毁灭性要大得多。1953年12月，一道总统指令切断了他接触机密文件的途径。

From 12 April to 6 May 1954 he was cross-examined at a security hearing, an administrative proceeding, not a criminal prosecution. On 29 June 1954 the Atomic Energy Commission voted 4 to 1 against letting him regain his clearance, two days before his consultancy contract was due to expire. The ruling did not find him disloyal, but nonetheless questioned his character and associates.

1954年4月12日至5月6日，他在一场安全听证会上接受盘问。这场听证会属于行政程序，而不是刑事起诉。1954年6月29日，原子能委员会以4比1投票，决定不让他恢复安全许可；两天之后，他的咨询合同就要到期。裁决没有认定他不忠，但仍然质疑他的品格和交往对象。

On 2 December 1963 President Johnson bestowed on him the Fermi Award, which Kennedy had authorised before his assassination. Oppenheimer died on 18 February 1967. In December 2022 the energy secretary officially vacated the 1954 determination, calling the process flawed and contrary to the commission's rules. The stigma of the revocation had persisted for fifty-five years beyond his lifetime.

1963年12月2日，约翰逊总统授予他费米奖；肯尼迪在遇刺前已批准了这一奖项。奥本海默于1967年2月18日去世。2022年12月，能源部长正式撤销了1954年的裁定，称当年的程序存在缺陷，违背了委员会的规定。吊销安全许可留下的污名，在他的一生结束后又延续了五十五年。

考研英语精读 · No.70

The Stamp That Changed the Post

一枚邮票改变通信

Before 1840, British postage was levied according to the number of sheets in a letter and the mileage of its carriage. The sum was usually payable on delivery, so the person receiving the correspondence, not the sender, settled it. Prices thus fluctuated from one postal item to the next.

1840年以前，英国的邮费按信件的页数和运送里程收取。这笔钱通常在投递时支付，因此付钱的是收到信件的人，而不是寄信人。价格也就随每件邮政物品而波动。

In 1837 Rowland Hill published a booklet urging reform. Parliament enacted the campaigner's proposal in 1839, and on 10 January 1840 the Uniform Penny Post began. A letter of up to half an ounce now cost one penny to any destination in Britain, and the fee was prepaid by whoever sent it. One flat-rate charge replaced variation with simplicity.

1837年，罗兰·希尔出版了一本小册子，呼吁改革。1839年，议会将这位改革倡导者的提议立法通过；1840年1月10日，"统一便士邮资"开始实行。重量不超过半盎司的信寄往英国境内任何目的地，都只需一便士，费用由寄信的一方预付。统一的单一费率以简便取代了差异。

The Penny Black, a one-penny denomination, was released on 1 May 1840, its validity starting on 6 May. Engraved on it was a youthful likeness of Queen Victoria, the reigning monarch. An aggregate of 68,808,000 were printed. The sheets had no holes for separation, so each stamp was cut out individually with scissors.

"黑便士"是一枚一便士面值的邮票，于1840年5月1日发行，有效期从5月6日开始。上面雕刻着当时在位的国君维多利亚女王年轻的肖像。印制总数为68,808,000枚。整版邮票之间没有用于分隔的孔，每枚都要用剪刀逐一剪下。

Mailings doubled, from about 80 million items in 1839 to over 169 million in 1840. The design had a drawback. Against black, the red cancelling mark was barely noticeable, lacking contrast, and the ink could be erased, leaving stamps vulnerable to reuse. In February 1841 the Penny Red, cancelled in black ink, arrived to deter tampering and fraud.

寄送的邮件翻了一番，从1839年的约8,000万件邮品增至1840年的1.69亿件以上。这一设计有个缺点。在黑色底上，红色注销戳几乎不显眼，缺乏反差，墨迹又能被擦除，邮票容易遭到重复使用。1841年2月，改用黑色墨迹注销的"红便士"问世，以防篡改和欺诈。

As the initiator of postage stamps, Britain set a precedent and earned an exemption. Its stamps are nameless, with no country inscribed on them. By international agreement, the sovereign's head suffices for identification, an implicit designation that other countries spell out in print.

作为邮票的首创者，英国开创了先例，并赢得一项豁免。英国邮票是无名的，上面没有刻印国名。根据国际协议，君主头像就足以作为识别标志，这是一种隐含的标识，其他国家则把国名印在邮票上。
